# MindBoost — Cumulative Audit Ledger

> **Canonical, append-only, single-writer.** This is the source of truth for MindBoost audit
> continuity. Read it at the START of any task and update it BEFORE declaring any task done.
> The rule that governs this file is persisted in Claude's memory graph as
> `mindboost-audit-ledger-protocol` and pointed to from [`CLAUDE.md`](CLAUDE.md).

---

## 0. Canonical status

- **Purpose:** one cumulative register of every finding/invariant across MindBoost, with immutable event
  history and per-task checkpoints, protected by a **self-consistency checksum** (§8). Prevents findings
  from silently disappearing between sessions, agents, and phases. NOTE: the §0 checksum detects
  ACCIDENTAL divergence, not adversarial tampering — real tamper-resistance needs an external anchor
  (signed git commit/tag or CI-published hash); see `MB-LEDGER-0003`.
- **Owner (single writer):** the primary Claude Code session. Subagents report structured findings;
  they do NOT edit this file concurrently. The owner reconciles their results. Single-writer is NOT yet
  mechanically enforced — see `MB-LEDGER-0001` (two live concurrent-write collisions observed this task).
- **Canonical path:** `MINDBOOST_AUDIT_LEDGER.md` (repository root). Absolute host path removed from this
  shared file for username privacy (`MB-LEDGER-0003`).

- **Prior checkpoint (S3 Tracer B):** `TASK-2026-07-20-FOCUS-V5-S3-TRACER-B` **Focus V5 slice S3 — the tracer is built end to end, and its exit gate rejected it.** The four tasks S3-A left untouched landed: a PURE reducer with the state machine as the only authority on legality, fail-closed restore with 10 typed rejections (one more than the plan asked for — absent and unreadable are different facts and were being collapsed), a write-ahead store that never returns a bare `Unit`, the 16-event ownership map, an accessibility-first screen with NATIVE per-platform target minima, and clock property tests. **All three reviewers returned NO APTO and all three were right.** Two blockers were defects in code written this same session: a restored session computed a negative latency and recorded it as an ANTICIPATION, manufacturing a claim about the person out of a process death (`MB-FOCUS-0022`); and the response window was written into every record while the engine enforced nothing, with no timer to expire it, so an unanswered trial stayed open forever and a session could not end (`MB-FOCUS-0023`). The third was a violation of an explicit standing prohibition — **raw per-trial data persisted with no production purge path** (`MB-FOCUS-0024`) — and the cheap fix was refused: the per-trial type was DELETED and the snapshot now holds per-condition counts, so the rule is enforced by the type system rather than by remembering to call `discard()`. **Declared loss:** per-trial latency is no longer recoverable after the fact. The diff audit also found the S2 oracle`s under-powered test was vacuous (`MB-FOCUS-0025`), green because its fixture tripped an earlier guard. ## 1. Governance rules

1. **IDs are permanent and never reused.** An ID maps to one invariant forever. Do not rename an
   existing ID onto a different problem. Legacy IDs with `P0`/`P1` embedded are preserved as-is and
   recorded as aliases in §6.
2. **No row is ever deleted.** History is append-only. Correcting a past mistake means adding a
   `CORRECTION` / `RETRACTION` event (§3), never editing the wrong entry away.
3. **Allowed states:** `OPEN`, `IN_PROGRESS`, `MITIGATED`, `BLOCKED`, `CLOSED`, `REOPENED`, `SUPERSEDED`.
4. **Severity ≠ state ≠ evidence.** They are three orthogonal columns. Severity does not appear in the
   ID (it can change). Severity is not downgraded without new evidence.
5. **Epistemic classification (separate axis):** `VERIFIED`, `CLAIMED_BY_CLAUDE`, `INFERRED`,
   `NOT_VERIFIED`, `CONTRADICTED`. A green targeted test is not `VERIFIED` of the whole system.
6. **`MITIGATED` ≠ `CLOSED`. `BLOCKED` ≠ `CLOSED`.** Do not swap `OPEN`→`BLOCKED` to dodge ownership.
7. **`SUPERSEDED` must point to the new ID** (§6). A `SUPERSEDED` without a destination is invalid.
8. **A closed finding can be reopened.** State changes preserve history.
9. **Closure hierarchy of evidence (§5):** narrative from Claude never substitutes for code, diff,
   discriminating test, log, or artifact. A review applies ONLY to the diff/hash it examined. A green
   test closes a regression only if it is discriminating (fails on the bug/mutation).
10. **Mandatory update every task** — implementations, mini-phases, one-line fixes, refactors, docs,
    tests, read-only reviews, research, diagnostics, migrations, prompt/governance changes, no-code
    tasks, blocked/cancelled/interrupted tasks, and subagent work. No task is `DONE`/`COMPLETE`/`APTO`/
    `CLOSED`/`VERIFIED`/frozen/ready-for-next-phase until §8's checklist passes.
11. **No new findings invented.** A task with no new finding records a `NO_NEW_FINDINGS` checkpoint (§4)
    naming the scope reviewed, evidence inspected, and prior findings potentially affected.
12. **Single writer.** The primary session owns writes; subagents propose, the owner reconciles.
13. **Never store secrets or personal data** in this ledger (no credentials, tokens, cognitive payloads,
    cleartext PII, or un-minimized user identifiers).
14. **Targeted ≠ full integration.** A subset test run is `REPORTED_GREEN`, not `TEST_VERIFIED`. Only a
    full suite green over a frozen hash, with diff+XML+logs attached, is `TEST_VERIFIED`.
15. **Implementation ≠ mitigation ≠ closure.** Landing code is `CLAIMED_BY_CLAUDE`; reducing risk is
    `MITIGATED`; closing requires the finding's closure gate satisfied with evidence (§5).

| `MB-LEDGER-0005` | **Corrupt-recovery multi-winner — mutual exclusion CONTRADICTED (V2d P6-R2, externally reported, reproduced worse):** the V2d P6 lock (`f5575b3f…`) recovered a CORRUPT lock via `read → sleep(grace) → unlinkSync(lockPath)`. There is NO identity/CAS between that read and the unlink, so a contender acting on a STALE corrupt observation deletes the VALID lock another contender just created, and both proceed. Reproduced at 100×24: **C1 empty-corrupt multi=28/100 rounds, C2 truncated-corrupt multi=47/100** (external auditor saw 2/30 at 30 rounds); C3 showed a contender deleting a legitimate slow creator's lock and winning. **Test blind-spot (distinct sub-finding):** every prior suite stayed GREEN throughout — `lock-tests.mjs` only raced the ABSENT (T1) and EXPIRED (T3) generations and never raced a CORRUPT generation at all, so 100% of the corrupt path was unraced while being claimed safe. | `MB-PORTFOLIO-0018` | **The Phase 4 fail-before harness crashed mid-probe, poisoned its own baseline, and the next run reported a GREEN restore against the corrupted bytes.** The first execution threw inside probe F4-1 (a real defect in my probe: `after` was already the counts object but was dereferenced as `after.counts`) **after** it had mutated `FOCUS-01` (`implementationStatus` LIVE_REACHABLE→HIDDEN, `mechanicImplemented` true→false) **and after** it had run `build-portfolio.mjs`, which rewrote the Markdown from the mutated CSV. The `restore()` call sat after the throw, so the CSV and MD were left mutated and the `.p4bak` backups were left on disk. The second execution then `backup()`-ed the CORRUPTED files over those backups, adopted `csv 75bee9ec…` as "the pre-Phase-4 snapshot", and printed `RESTORATION: byte-identical = true` — true, but relative to a poisoned baseline. F4-1 consequently read `HIDDEN→HIDDEN` and reported NOT REPRODUCED. A mutation harness without a fail-closed baseline check is a corruption vector that certifies its own corruption. | P1 | CLOSED | VERIFIED (run 1 pre-snapshot `csv 7f84eff3…` vs run 2 pre-snapshot `csv 75bee9ec…`; `FOCUS-01` on disk read `impl HIDDEN mech false` with liveReachable 42 while the published MD said 43; both files are untracked so git offered no recovery) · area:portfolio/tooling | **FIXED (P4).** Recovery: the immutable V2c handoff ZIP (`0f8e2f92…`) contains the CSV and MD; both were extracted and their hashes matched the `SHA256SUMS.txt` pins **exactly** (`7f84eff3…`, `1679c7d6…`) before being restored — recovery by verification, not by reconstruction. This is the first time the immutability rule on the V2c bundle paid a debt. Harness hardened with two guards that make the failure impossible: (1) `preflight()` hash-checks every mutable input against its `SHA256SUMS.txt` pin and aborts (exit 2) on any mismatch or stray `.p4bak`, so a dirty baseline can never be silently adopted; (2) `probe(files, fn)` wraps every mutation in try/finally, so a throw can never leave a mutation on disk. Both guards are also built into the Phase 4 mutation oracle (`phase4-mutation-tests.mjs`), which additionally aborts if the baseline already has a failing BLOCKING gate — otherwise every mutation below it would be vacuous. Re-run after hardening: **7/7 reproduced**, all bytes restored byte-identical. | primary session | a harness that mutates files must be fail-closed about its OWN baseline: hash-pin the inputs before touching them, mutate only inside try/finally, and refuse to run on a dirty tree — "restored byte-identical" is meaningless if the baseline was already poisoned; untracked authority files need an immutable off-tree copy or they are unrecoverable | `MB-LEDGER-0006` | none — both guards are in the two Phase 4 harnesses; other suites (Phase 2/3) run in sandboxes and never write to the repo (P3-R) | 2026-07-15 V2d-P4 | 2026-07-15 V2d-P4 |

| `MB-FOCUS-0015` | **The loglinear (Hautus 1995) correction biases d' when the signal and noise cells differ in size, so a pure yes-sayer lands at a d' that is NOT zero.** On the vigilance tracer (24 signal vs 96 noise) someone who responds to every stimulus gives H = 24.5/25 = 0.980 and F = 96.5/97 = 0.995 — the larger cell is corrected less — so d' = **-0.51**, comfortably outside any near-zero band. The first draft of the candidate scorer decided "this session discriminated nothing" by testing `abs(d')` against a threshold, and would have called that session VALID. | P1 | CLOSED | VERIFIED (predicted by hand from the correction's algebra, then reproduced empirically in the S2 simulation: `E01 MASHER d'=-0.511`) | **FIXED in S2, before any engine was committed — which is what task (5) exists for.** The verdict no longer relies on an `abs(d')` band: a session in which exactly ONE distinct response was emitted across every scorable trial is `Invalidated(DEGENERATE_RESPONDING)`, derived from RESPONSE COUNTS ONLY. Deliberately not from latency uniformity — the more obvious signature, but reading it would violate the ratified rule that RT is secondary, and assertion A5 pins that it is not read. The artifact is NOT tuned away: it is the disclosed price of applying the correction unconditionally, which is what keeps d' always finite. | psychometrician | S2 simulation | `RAT-FOCUS-S2-RULES` | Android/Desktop/iOS | 2026-07-20 (Focus V5 S2) | 2026-07-20 |

| `MB-LEDGER-0007` | **The primary session destroyed this ledger with `sed`, and the ledger's own canonical hash was unreproducible on a fresh Windows checkout.** Three defects surfaced together. (a) **Self-inflicted, now recovered:** an in-place `sed -i` substitution meant to remove a markdown-breaking absolute-value notation, whose pattern wrapped `d'` in the two-character sequence backslash-then-pipe. In GNU sed BRE that sequence is the ALTERNATION operator, not an escaped pipe, so the pattern reduced to "empty OR d-prime OR empty": the empty branch matched at EVERY position and inserted the replacement between every character (196 KB grew to 2,066,328 B), while the other branch CONSUMED every literal `d'` in the file. Reversing the insertion recovered ASCII only — `sed` had decoded the file as UTF-8, hit invalid sequences created by its own insertions, and emitted U+FFFD per byte: **3446 characters destroyed across 272 lines**, plus every literal `d'` silently deleted by the alternation. (b) **Pre-existing, latent:** `core.autocrlf=true` with no `.gitattributes` materialises this LF-hashed file as CRLF on checkout, so `scripts/validate-audit-ledger.mjs` reported `hash mismatch` on a PRISTINE tree — the validator was structurally unable to pass on this host. (c) **The defect the `sed` was chasing was real:** `MB-FOCUS-0015` carried markdown-escaped pipes, which the validator's naive split on the pipe character reads as extra columns, so cell 4 resolved to prose and the row was rejected as an invalid state. Recording (c) is itself evidence of how sharp the trap is: the FIRST draft of this very row reintroduced the same break, at 20 cells instead of 14, by spelling the offending characters literally while describing them. | P1 | CLOSED | VERIFIED (byte-level: `od -c` confirmed em-dash `342 200 224` became three U+FFFD; run-length analysis over 270 residual holes; **cryptographic proof of exact recovery** — the reconstruction independently reproduces stamped hash `2d324081…4da995`, the hash sealed by the last pre-incident command, so the restored content is byte-identical to the pre-incident ledger) | **RECOVERED WITHOUT INFERENCE, and that distinction is the point.** The corrupt copy was NOT repaired by guessing characters from context; reconstructing an audit record by inference is the fabrication this ledger exists to prevent. Instead: (1) corrupt copy preserved as evidence before any write; (2) `git checkout HEAD` restored the committed base, which contained ZERO `MB-FOCUS` rows because R0/S0/S1/S2 were all uncommitted working-tree state; (3) the 30 ledger `Edit`/`Write` calls were extracted VERBATIM from the session transcript and replayed in order (29 applied; #25 had failed on an ambiguous anchor in the original session too, and #26 was its successful retry); (4) the single content-mutating shell command was replayed; (5) a skeleton diff against the corrupt copy confirmed 466/466 line agreement. (a) fixed by never running `sed` against a file with non-ASCII content; (b) fixed by a `.gitattributes` entry pinning this file to `text eol=lf`; (c) fixed by replacing the escaped pipes with `abs(d')`. | primary session | validator GREEN on a clean tree (`LEDGER OK`, 92 findings) AND the stamped hash independently recomputed after re-reading the file | `MB-LEDGER-0001` (single-writer), `MB-LEDGER-0003` (external anchor) | host tooling (Windows/NTFS/Git Bash GNU sed); POSIX NOT re-run | 2026-07-20 (Focus V5 S2 close-out) | 2026-07-20 |

| `MB-FOCUS-0022` | **A session restored after process death computed a NEGATIVE latency and turned it into an anticipation — a behavioural claim about the person, manufactured by the operating system killing the app.** `MonotonicFocusClock` takes its zero mark when the object is CONSTRUCTED, so a snapshot whose `presentedAtElapsedNanos` came from the previous process is measured against a clock that restarted. A trial presented 300 s into the old process and answered 400 ms into the new one yields 400 ms minus 300 s, and the negative difference falls below the 150 ms anticipation floor, so the engine records ANTICIPATION: the person answered before perception was possible. Nothing about that is true. It also survives into the tallies, where it is indistinguishable from a real anticipation. | P1 | CLOSED | VERIFIED by the efficacy gate in fresh context, then reproduced as a failing test before the fix (`FocusSessionRestoreTest` + `FocusSessionReducerTest`). Layer (b) did not fail closed, it failed OPEN: with a dead-epoch anchor `atElapsedNanos <= deadline` is trivially satisfied and a null latency disables the anticipation floor, so the two guards COMPOSED into crediting a clean HIT to someone who may have deliberated for minutes inside a 1200 ms window — absence fabricating a MAXIMUM, the 2026-07-15 learning in its worst direction. The test I wrote pinned that wrong behaviour with the argument that only the latency had been lost. The argument was wrong: what is lost is whether there was any answer INSIDE the window. ## 5. Closure evidence requirements

A finding may move to `CLOSED` only when ALL of:

- an identifiable correction exists;
- a discriminating test exists where applicable (fails on the regression or an equivalent mutation);
- the command AND output are attached;
- the verified platforms are named;
- code, tests, docs, and this ledger agree;
- the final reviewer examined the SAME diff/hash;
- no known caller or bypass remains;
- the finding's closure gate (§2 column) is satisfied.

If anything is missing, it stays `OPEN`, `MITIGATED`, or `BLOCKED`.

## 8. Ledger validation checklist (run before ending EVERY task)

- [ ] Read the ledger before working.
- [ ] Reviewed every finding the task's delta could affect.
- [ ] Added new findings with a unique ID.
- [ ] Did not delete or hide any prior finding.
- [ ] Justified any severity change with evidence.
- [ ] Attached evidence for each state change.
- [ ] Closed nothing on narrative evidence alone.
- [ ] Recorded reviewers and the exact snapshot/hash.
- [ ] Recorded unverified platforms.
- [ ] Appended the immutable event(s) (§3).
- [ ] Appended the task checkpoint (§4).
- [ ] Checked code ↔ tests ↔ docs ↔ ledger for contradictions.
- [ ] Confirmed no secrets or personal data included.
- [ ] Recomputed and recorded the current ledger hash.

### Hash convention (self-excluding)

The **Canonical SHA-256** in §0 is computed over the entire file **with the single line matching
`^Canonical SHA-256`** removed. To verify from the repo root:

```bash
grep -v '^Canonical SHA-256' MINDBOOST_AUDIT_LEDGER.md | sha256sum
```

The 64-hex output must equal the value stamped in §0. A lightweight validator lives at
[`scripts/validate-audit-ledger.mjs`](scripts/validate-audit-ledger.mjs) (unique IDs, valid states,
`CLOSED`⇒evidence+gate, `SUPERSEDED`⇒destination, 64-hex hash present & matching, no obvious secrets,
a checkpoint exists). Run: `node scripts/validate-audit-ledger.mjs`.

**This is a self-consistency checksum, not a tamper-resistant one.** The same writer recomputes and
re-stamps it, so it catches ACCIDENTAL divergence (concurrent writes, a forgotten recompute), NOT an
adversarial edit that also updates the stamp. Real tamper-resistance needs an EXTERNAL anchor — a signed
git commit/tag or a CI-published hash (`MB-LEDGER-0003`). Because single-writer is not mechanically
enforced (`MB-LEDGER-0001`), the §0 count+hash MUST be reconciled LAST (after all row appends) and
re-validated by re-reading the file before the task is declared done.

---

## 9. ID policy (reference)

- Preserve every ID already in §39/§40, Decision Log, and prior audits. Never reassign an existing ID.
- **New** findings use `MB-<AREA>-<NNNN>` (severity NOT in the ID), e.g. `MB-ERASURE-0001`. Existing
  canonical IDs in §2 are the source-doc's stable non-severity slugs — kept as-is; their `P0-x` labels
  are recorded as aliases in §6.
- Two symptoms share one ID only if they share invariant + owner + fix + gate; otherwise separate IDs.
